❯ now we start with new project Performance & Resiliency Architectural Upgrades 
  we work in folder LAB4\EXERICE, I will give you 4 PRDs resoectively, then you 
  cook it                                                                       
  PRD 01 — Route-Level Code Splitting & Lazy Loading                            
  Commit: feat(perf): implement PersonDetail lazy loading                       
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
     Implement dynamic component loading for PersonDetail.tsx using React's     
  lazy() API.                                                                   
                                                                                
  Instead of loading the PersonDetail component immediately when the            
  application starts, React should download its JavaScript module only when the 
  corresponding component is required.                                          
                                                                                
  This reduces the initial JavaScript bundle size and improves initial          
  application loading performance.                                              
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
     2.1 Create PersonDetail Component                                          
     Implement or preserve the existing PersonDetail.tsx component.             
                                                                                
  Maintain its existing functionality and component interface.                  
                                                                                
  Ensure that the component uses a default export so it can be consumed by      
  React.lazy().                                                                 
                                                                                
  2.2 Implement Dynamic Import                                                  
  Import lazy from React.                                                       
                                                                                
  Replace the static import of PersonDetail with a dynamic import.              
                                                                                
  The dynamic import must reference the actual location of PersonDetail.tsx.    
                                                                                
  Expected implementation pattern:                                              
                                                                                
  import { lazy } from 'react';                                                 
                                                                                
  const PersonDetail = lazy(() =>                                               
    import('./PersonDetail')                                                    
  );                                                                            
                                                                                
  export default PersonDetail;                                                  
  2.3 Code Splitting Requirements                                               
  The PersonDetail module must be separated into an asynchronous JavaScript     
  chunk.                                                                        
                                                                                
  The component must not be eagerly imported elsewhere in the route-loading     
  implementation.                                                               
                                                                                
  Preserve the existing component behavior.                                     
                                                                                
  Avoid unnecessary changes to unrelated components.                            
                                                                                
  3. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  PersonDetail.tsx exists and exports its component correctly.                  
                                                                                
  React.lazy() is used for dynamic importing.                                   
                                                                                
  The application successfully compiles.                                        
                                                                                
  The PersonDetail component is loaded asynchronously.                          
                                                                                
  Production build generates a separate chunk for the lazy-loaded component.    
                                                                                
  Existing PersonDetail functionality remains intact.                           
                                                                                
  4. Out of Scope                                                               
     Implementing the Suspense fallback.                                        
                                                                                
  Creating skeleton loading animations.                                         
                                                                                
  Modifying unrelated application routes.                                       
                                                                                
  Rebuilding the existing routing architecture.

● PRD 01 is done and committed as 19ccf2b feat(perf): implement PersonDetail 
  lazy loading. The production build puts PersonDetail in its own 0.74 kB file
  that only downloads when it's needed. All 7 tests pass and the TypeScript
  check and build both succeed.

  LAB4/EXERCISE was empty, so I set up a new React 19 + TypeScript + Vite
  project there: a small "People Directory" app with a list of people that opens
  a profile when you click one.

  What changed:
  - src/components/PersonDetail.tsx: the profile view (name, role, email,
    location, bio, skills), using a default export as the PRD asks.
  - src/components/LazyPersonDetail.tsx: uses exactly the pattern from the PRD,
    const PersonDetail = lazy(() => import("./PersonDetail")); export default 
    PersonDetail;.
  - src/App.tsx: imports only the lazy version. No other file loads
    ./PersonDetail directly, and a test checks this.

  One thing you'd otherwise trip over: there's no Suspense fallback yet, since
  the PRD leaves it out of scope. To make that safe, I wrap the click that
  selects a person in startTransition. While the file downloads, the current
  screen stays up instead of breaking. A proper loading state is probably coming
  in a later PRD.

  How it's checked:
  - npm test runs the 7 tests. They cover:
    - the profile shows all its fields
    - the lazy version really is React.lazy and shows the same profile once
      loaded
    - the app starts with no profile and loads one when you click a person
    - only LazyPersonDetail.tsx imports ./PersonDetail
  - npm run verify:chunks builds for production and fails if PersonDetail ends
    up in the main bundle instead of its own file.

  The commit only contains LAB4/EXERCISE. Your uncommitted edits to the two LAB3
  index.html files are untouched. I didn't write a report, same as for LAB3.

  Ready for PRD 02.

※ recap: You're working through four performance PRDs in LAB4/EXERCISE, and PRD 01 (lazy-loading PersonDetail) is built, tested and committed. Next, send me PRD 02 so I can start on it. (turn off recaps in /config)

❯ PRD 02 — Suspense Boundaries & Resilient Route Loading                        
  Commit: feat(ui): implement Suspense boundaries for lazy routes               
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
  Introduce React Suspense boundaries around the lazy-loaded PersonDetail       
  component.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Since lazy-loaded components require asynchronous module resolution, the      
  application must provide a fallback interface while the component is being    
  downloaded.                                                                   
                                                                                
  The goal is to prevent an incomplete or blank interface from appearing during 
  asynchronous component loading.                                               
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
  2.1 Implement Suspense                                                        
  Import Suspense from React.                                                   
                                                                                
  Wrap the lazy-loaded PersonDetail component inside a Suspense boundary.       
                                                                                
  Provide a fallback UI while the component is loading.                         
                                                                                
  Expected implementation pattern:                                              
                                                                                
  import { Suspense } from 'react';                                             
  import PersonDetail from './PersonDetail';                                    
                                                                                
  <Suspense fallback={<div>Loading...</div>}>                                   
    <PersonDetail />                                                            
  </Suspense>                                                                   
  The example above demonstrates the required structure. The temporary fallback 
  will be replaced with the dedicated Skeleton component in PRD 03.             
                                                                                
  2.2 Route Integration                                                         
  Integrate the Suspense boundary into the existing route configuration.        
                                                                                
  Ensure that the boundary only affects the intended lazy-loaded route.         
                                                                                
  Preserve existing route paths and navigation behavior.                        
                                                                                
  Avoid introducing unnecessary global Suspense boundaries.                     
                                                                                
  2.3 Loading Behavior                                                          
  Display fallback content while the lazy module is being resolved.             
                                                                                
  Automatically render PersonDetail once loading completes.                     
                                                                                
  Ensure the fallback disappears when the actual component becomes available.   
                                                                                
  3. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  Suspense is imported from React.                                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  PersonDetail is wrapped inside a Suspense boundary.                           
                                                                                
                                                                                
  A fallback interface appears during lazy loading.                             
                                                                                
                                                                                
  PersonDetail renders successfully after loading.                              
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Existing navigation and route behavior remain unchanged.                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  No blank loading screen is displayed for the intended route.                  
                                                                                
  4. Out of Scope                                                               
  Implementing the final Skeleton UI.                                           
                                                                                
  Adding custom loading animations.                                             
                                                                                
  Changing the routing library.                                                 
                                                                                
  Implementing application-wide error handling.                                 
                                                                                
  PRD 03 — Dimension-Matched Skeleton & Zero Layout Shift                       
  Commit: feat(ui): implement dimension-matched loading skeleton                
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
  Replace the temporary Suspense fallback with a dedicated Skeleton loading     
  component.                                                                    
                                                                                
  The Skeleton must resemble the layout dimensions of the actual PersonDetail   
  component.                                                                    
                                                                                
  The primary objective is to prevent Cumulative Layout Shift (CLS) while the   
  lazy-loaded component is being downloaded.                                    
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
  2.1 Create Skeleton Component                                                 
  Create a dedicated Skeleton component.                                        
                                                                                
  Reproduce the approximate structure of the PersonDetail loading interface.    
                                                                                
  Use CSS to implement placeholder elements.                                    
                                                                                
  Ensure that the skeleton occupies approximately the same dimensions as the    
  final component.                                                              
                                                                                
  2.2 Dimension-Matched Layout                                                  
  The Skeleton must reserve sufficient space for the actual content.            
                                                                                
  Requirements:                                                                 
                                                                                
  Match the overall component width.                                            
                                                                                
  Match the expected component height.                                          
                                                                                
  Preserve the spacing between major content sections.                          
                                                                                
  Reserve space for profile images or other visual elements if present.         
                                                                                
  Avoid sudden layout expansion when PersonDetail finishes loading.             
                                                                                
  2.3 Loading Animation                                                         
  Implement a lightweight CSS-based pulsing or shimmer animation.               
                                                                                
  Use CSS animations instead of JavaScript timers.                              
                                                                                
  Ensure the animation does not interfere with the application's                
  responsiveness.                                                               
                                                                                
  Respect the user's reduced-motion preference.                                 
                                                                                
  2.4 Integrate Skeleton with Suspense                                          
  Replace the temporary fallback from PRD 02.                                   
                                                                                
  Expected implementation pattern:                                              
                                                                                
  <Suspense fallback={<PersonDetailSkeleton />}>                                
    <PersonDetail />                                                            
  </Suspense>                                                                   
  3. Performance Requirements                                                   
  The fallback must reserve layout space before PersonDetail renders.           
                                                                                
  Avoid unnecessary DOM changes during loading.                                 
                                                                                
  Target CLS = 0 for the tested lazy-loading transition.                        
                                                                                
  Do not introduce large images or additional dependencies solely for the       
  skeleton.                                                                     
                                                                                
  4. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  A dedicated Skeleton component exists.                                        
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Skeleton dimensions correspond to PersonDetail.                               
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Suspense uses the Skeleton as its fallback.                                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  A loading animation is implemented using CSS.                                 
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Reduced-motion preferences are respected.                                     
                                                                                
                                                                                
  No noticeable layout jump occurs when loading completes.                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  CLS target of 0 is achieved in the controlled loading test.                   
                                                                                
  5. Out of Scope                                                               
  Rebuilding the PersonDetail interface.                                        
                                                                                
  Introducing third-party skeleton libraries.                                   
                                                                                
  Adding unrelated loading animations to the application.                       
  go on with second and third  PRDs

● PRDs 02 and 03 are done and committed, each as its own commit. The skeleton
  swaps to the real profile with zero layout shift for all 3 people, on desktop
  (1280px) and phone (375px). All 12 unit tests and 10 browser tests pass, and
  the build check still finds PersonDetail in its own file.

  PRD 02, dd635ca: Suspense boundary
  - There's exactly one <Suspense>, wrapped only around the PersonDetail spot in
    App.tsx. The header and the people list stay on screen while the profile
    loads. There's no app-wide boundary, and main.tsx doesn't have one.
  - The app has no router, so I didn't add one. The boundary sits where the
    "route" would be.
  - I removed the PRD 01 startTransition workaround, because it would have
    hidden the loading fallback this PRD asks for.
  - The test (src/App.suspense.test.tsx) holds PersonDetail's module back until
    the test releases it. It checks that the fallback shows while loading, the
    profile replaces it, and switching people afterwards doesn't flash the
    fallback again.

  PRD 03, c0f515b: skeleton with no layout jump
  - The component: PersonDetailSkeleton.tsx has the same 4 sections as the real
    profile (header, email/location, bio, skills). Both use the same new CSS
    file, personDetailLayout.css, where every row has a fixed height.
  - Something you'd notice visually: on a phone, Le Thu Ha's skills wrap onto a
    second row, which made the real profile taller than the skeleton. So the
    skills area now always reserves two rows, the same way the bio always
    reserves 3 lines. On desktop, profiles with one row of skills have a little
    extra space at the bottom.
  - Animation: a CSS-only opacity pulse, no JavaScript timers. It stops when the
    user's system has reduced motion turned on.
  - Loads in time: the skeleton's CSS is in the main stylesheet that loads with
    the page, so the space is reserved before PersonDetail's file arrives.

  How the "CLS = 0" was checked: new browser tests (npm run test:e2e) run the
  production build in your installed Microsoft Edge, the same setup as LAB3. For
  each person they hold back PersonDetail's file and measure the skeleton. Then
  they release the file and check two things: the real profile has the exact
  same position and size, and the browser recorded zero layout shift.

  There was one weakness in that test, which I fixed. Nothing sits below the
  profile on the page, so a wrong-sized skeleton wouldn't have moved anything
  and the shift count would stay 0 regardless. The test now adds a small block
  under the profile. I also deliberately broke the skeleton once to confirm the
  test catches a shift (it recorded 0.005).

  Your LAB3 edits are still uncommitted and untouched. Ready for PRD 04.

  PRD 04 — Accessible Loading States & Final Integration                        
  Commit: feat(a11y): implement accessible loading state                        
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
     Improve the accessibility and resilience of the lazy-loading experience.   
                                                                                
  The loading interface must communicate its current state to assistive         
  technologies through appropriate ARIA attributes.                             
                                                                                
  The final implementation must integrate code splitting, Suspense boundaries,  
  Skeleton fallbacks, and accessibility into one coherent workflow.             
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
     2.1 Implement aria-busy                                                    
     Add aria-busy="true" to the appropriate loading region.                    
                                                                                
  Communicate that the content is currently being updated.                      
                                                                                
  Ensure the attribute accurately reflects the loading state.                   
                                                                                
  Restore aria-busy="false" when the content is ready.                          
                                                                                
  2.2 Implement aria-live                                                       
  Add aria-live="polite" to the loading status region.                          
                                                                                
  Ensure loading updates can be communicated to screen readers.                 
                                                                                
  Avoid unnecessary repeated announcements.                                     
                                                                                
  Do not use aggressive announcements that interrupt the user's current         
  activity.                                                                     
                                                                                
  2.3 Accessible Skeleton                                                       
  Ensure decorative skeleton elements are hidden from assistive technologies    
  where appropriate.                                                            
                                                                                
  Provide an accessible loading status.                                         
                                                                                
  Preserve meaningful semantic HTML.                                            
                                                                                
  Ensure the loading interface does not interfere with keyboard navigation.     
                                                                                
  2.4 Final Integration                                                         
  Verify the complete loading workflow:                                         
                                                                                
  User navigates to the PersonDetail route.                                     
                                                                                
  React requests the lazy-loaded JavaScript module.                             
                                                                                
  Suspense detects that the component is not ready.                             
                                                                                
  The Skeleton fallback is displayed.                                           
                                                                                
  The loading region communicates its busy state.                               
                                                                                
  PersonDetail finishes loading.                                                
                                                                                
  The Skeleton disappears.                                                      
                                                                                
  The actual PersonDetail component is rendered.                                
                                                                                
  The loading region returns to its non-busy state.                             
                                                                                
  3. Testing Requirements                                                       
     Perform the following checks:                                              
                                                                                
  Performance                                                                   
                                                                                
  Verify that PersonDetail is split into a separate production chunk.           
                                                                                
  Verify that the initial application bundle does not eagerly include the       
  component.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Inspect CLS during the loading transition.                                    
                                                                                
  Accessibility                                                                 
                                                                                
  Verify aria-busy behavior.                                                    
                                                                                
  Verify aria-live="polite".                                                    
                                                                                
  Inspect the accessibility tree.                                               
                                                                                
  Test keyboard navigation.                                                     
                                                                                
  Resiliency                                                                    
                                                                                
  Test the loading state using browser network throttling.                      
                                                                                
  Verify that the component renders after the module finishes downloading.      
                                                                                
  Verify that no console errors are introduced.                                 
                                                                                
  4. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  aria-busy is implemented correctly.                                           
                                                                                
  aria-live="polite" is implemented correctly.                                  
                                                                                
  Skeleton loading content is accessible.                                       
                                                                                
  Keyboard navigation remains functional.                                       
                                                                                
  PersonDetail loads successfully through the lazy-loading workflow.            
                                                                                
  No unexpected layout shifts occur.                                            
                                                                                
  No new console errors or warnings are introduced.                             
                                                                                
  Existing application functionality remains intact.                            
                                                                                
  5. Out of Scope                                                               
     Rebuilding the application's accessibility architecture.                   
                                                                                
  Implementing unrelated application features.                                  
                                                                                
  Replacing the existing routing system.                                        
                                                                                
  Introducing an unrelated state-management library.                            
                                                                                
  Final Deliverables                                                            
  The completed Exercise 1.1 must demonstrate the following architectural       
  improvements:                                                                 
                                                                                
  Requirement    Expected Result                                                
  Code Splitting    Dynamic imports through React.lazy()                        
  Lazy Loading    PersonDetail.tsx loads asynchronously                         
  Suspend Boundaries    Routes wrapped with Suspense                            
  Skeleton Fallback    Dimension-matched loading interface                      
  Layout Stability    Target CLS = 0                                            
  Accessibility    aria-busy="true" and aria-live="polite"                      
  Resiliency    Consistent loading-to-success transition                        
  Final Expected Architecture:                                                  
                                                                                
  src/                                                                          
  ├── components/                                                               
  │   ├── PersonDetail.tsx                                                      
  │   └── PersonDetailSkeleton.tsx                                              
  │                                                                             
  ├── routes/                                                                   
  │   └── ExistingRoutes.tsx                                                    
  │                                                                             
  └── ...                                                                       
  Note: Preserve the existing repository structure. The directory names above   
  are illustrative and should be adapted to the current project.                

● PRD 04's main a11y issue is where the announcements live. Right now the
  skeleton is the live region, and screen readers often stay silent when a live
  region is newly inserted. Plan:
  - A persistent profile region with aria-busy that switches true → false.
  - A persistent polite status line, outside the busy region (screen readers can
    hold back announcements from inside a busy region).
  - The skeleton becomes purely decorative (aria-hidden), so nothing gets
    announced twice.

● PRD 04 is done and committed as ee8d6b9 feat(a11y): implement accessible 
  loading state, so all 4 PRDs are finished. 13 unit tests and 16 browser tests
  pass (desktop and 375px phone), along with the TypeScript check and the chunk
  check.

  What changed for accessibility:
  - aria-busy: a new LoadingRegion component wraps the profile area in a region
    named "Profile" that's always on the page. Its aria-busy is "true" only
    while the skeleton is showing, and goes back to "false" when PersonDetail
    appears. The skeleton reports when it appears and disappears, so the
    attribute always matches what's on screen.
  - aria-live="polite": a status line, always on the page, says "Loading
    profile…" and then "Profile loaded.". I placed it outside the busy region,
    because screen readers can hold back announcements from inside a region
    marked busy. Switching to another person once the file is cached doesn't
    announce anything again.
  - Skeleton: now fully hidden from screen readers (aria-hidden), with nothing
    you can tab to. In PRD 03 the skeleton was itself the status area; I moved
    that job to the always-present status line so nothing is announced twice.

  PRD 04 test checklist:
  - Performance:
    - PersonDetail is in its own file and isn't downloaded until you pick a
      person.
    - The chunk script now also checks that the main bundle contains none of
      PersonDetail's code.
    - The layout shift during loading is 0, including on the slow network below.
  - Accessibility:
    - aria-busy goes true → false and the status is polite.
    - While loading, the accessibility tree shows only the empty "Profile"
      region; once loaded it shows the profile's heading, email link and skills.
    - axe finds no violations either while loading or after.
    - Keyboard: Enter and Space both select a person, focus stays on the button
      you pressed while loading, and Tab then reaches the profile's email link.
  - Resiliency:
    - With the network throttled in Edge (2-second delay, about 50 KB/s), the
      page goes skeleton → profile.
    - No console errors or warnings in the browser runs, and none from React's
      development mode in the unit tests.

  Structure: the PRD's sample layout shows a routes/ExistingRoutes.tsx file.
  This app has no router, so the Suspense boundary stays in App.tsx, as the
  PRD's own note allows.

  To rerun everything from LAB4/EXERCISE: npm test, npm run test:e2e, npm run 
  verify:chunks.

  Your LAB3 index.html edits are still uncommitted and untouched. I didn't write
  a report or log; tell me when you want one.

  PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS DOCUMENT                                                 
  Sprint 1 — Responsive Master-Detail Directory & Case Dossier Router           
  1. Sprint Overview                                                            
     Objective                                                                  
     Refactor the existing React application into a responsive Master-Detail    
  Directory using modern React Router v7 declarative syntax.                    
                                                                                
  The implementation must establish a shared layout architecture using nested   
  routes and React Router's <Outlet />, while supporting dynamic detail pages   
  through the useParams() hook.                                                 
                                                                                
  The application must provide a responsive two-column CSS Grid layout, safe    
  URL parameter extraction, robust invalid-ID handling, and semantic HTML for   
  structured person information.                                                
                                                                                
  Sprint Constraints                                                            
  React Router v7 must be used.                                                 
                                                                                
  Replace legacy <Switch> syntax with <Routes>.                                 
                                                                                
  Route definitions must use the element prop with JSX elements.                
                                                                                
  Nested routes must render through <Outlet />.                                 
                                                                                
  PeopleLayout.jsx must implement the shared master-detail layout.              
                                                                                
  PersonDetail.jsx must handle dynamic person details.                          
                                                                                
  Dynamic route parameters must be extracted using useParams().                 
                                                                                
  Person records must be located using people.find().                           
                                                                                
  Invalid IDs must render a 404/Not Found state.                                
                                                                                
  No console warnings or errors are allowed.                                    
                                                                                
  Preserve the existing project structure and functionality.                    
                                                                                
  PRD 01 — Declarative Router Syntax Migration                                  
  Development Task: Modernize the application's routing configuration.          
                                                                                
  Git Commit: Part of feat: router layout                                       
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
     Replace the legacy React Router <Switch> syntax with the modern            
  declarative <Routes> architecture.                                            
                                                                                
  Establish a clean route configuration that supports nested route definitions  
  and JSX-based element rendering.                                              
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
     2.1 Replace Switch with Routes                                             
     Remove the legacy <Switch> component.                                      
                                                                                
  Import <Routes> and <Route> from React Router v7.                             
                                                                                
  Use <Routes> as the top-level route container.                                
                                                                                
  Ensure all route definitions follow the modern matching algorithm.            
                                                                                
  2.2 Element Prop Syntax                                                       
  Define route components using the element prop.                               
                                                                                
  Pass JSX elements directly into route definitions.                            
                                                                                
  Avoid legacy component-based route declarations.                              
                                                                                
  Expected implementation pattern:                                              
                                                                                
  import { Routes, Route } from 'react-router';                                 
                                                                                
  function App() {                                                              
    return (                                                                    
      <Routes>                                                                  
        <Route                                                                  
          path="/"                                                              
          element={<PeopleLayout />}                                            
        >                                                                       
          {/* Nested routes will be added here */}                              
        </Route>                                                                
      </Routes>                                                                 
    );                                                                          
  }                                                                             
  2.3 Route Organization                                                        
  Prepare the route hierarchy for:                                              
                                                                                
  A shared PeopleLayout parent route.                                           
                                                                                
  A default/index route for the directory.                                      
                                                                                
  A dynamic detail route for individual people.                                 
                                                                                
  Do not introduce unrelated routes.                                            
                                                                                
  3. Technical Constraints                                                      
     Use React Router v7.                                                       
                                                                                
  Preserve the existing router provider and application entry point.            
                                                                                
  Avoid unnecessary dependencies.                                               
                                                                                
  Ensure route paths remain clean and predictable.                              
                                                                                
  4. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  Legacy <Switch> syntax is removed from the relevant routing configuration.    
                                                                                
  <Routes> is implemented.                                                      
                                                                                
  Routes use the element prop.                                                  
                                                                                
  JSX elements are passed directly into route definitions.                      
                                                                                
  The application compiles successfully.                                        
                                                                                
  No routing-related console warnings appear.                                   
                                                                                
  5. Out of Scope                                                               
     Implementing dynamic person-detail lookup.                                 
                                                                                
  Implementing the 404 state.                                                   
                                                                                
  Rebuilding the directory interface.                                           
                                                                                
  Changing unrelated application functionality.

● I moved the project to LAB4/EXERCISE/1.2; LAB4/HW is empty again. PRD 01 works
  there: all 6 tests pass and the production build succeeds.

  What PRD 01 contains:
  - src/App.jsx: one <Routes> block with 3 routes, each given a JSX element
    through the element prop. Everything is imported from react-router
    (v7.18.6):
    - <Route path="/" element={<PeopleLayout />}> is the shared parent.
    - <Route index element={<DirectoryIndex />} /> is the default directory
      view.
    - <Route path="people/:personId" element={<PersonDetail />} /> is the person
      detail page.
  - src/main.jsx: sets up <BrowserRouter> around <App />. App.jsx only declares
    the routes.
  - PeopleLayout.jsx: a heading plus <Outlet />. The two-column grid comes in a
    later PRD.
  - PersonDetail.jsx: a placeholder for now. Reading the URL id with useParams()
    and the people.find() lookup are out of scope for this PRD.

  Tests (src/App.test.jsx):
  - / shows the layout with the index view inside it, and /people/p-02 shows the
    detail page inside the same layout.
  - Any console.error or console.warn fails the test.
  - A guard test fails on legacy syntax: <Switch>, component=, render=, exact,
    react-router-dom, useHistory, withRouter and <Redirect>. It also requires
    all 3 routes to use element={<X />}, keeps the router setup in main.jsx, and
    checks that React Router v7 is the version installed.

  PRD 02 — Nested PeopleLayout Architecture & Responsive CSS Grid               
  Development Task: Implement the shared master-detail layout using React       
  Router nested routes.                                                         
                                                                                
  Git Commit: feat: router layout                                               
                                                                                
  1. Objective                                                                  
     Create PeopleLayout.jsx as the shared parent layout for the Master-Detail  
  Directory.                                                                    
                                                                                
  The layout must establish a responsive two-column CSS Grid structure and use  
  React Router's <Outlet /> to dynamically render child routes.                 
                                                                                
  The navigation interface must use <NavLink> with functional callback-based    
  active styling.                                                               
                                                                                
  2. Functional Requirements                                                    
     2.1 Create PeopleLayout.jsx                                                
     Create or update PeopleLayout.jsx.                                         
                                                                                
  Implement the shared navigation and master-detail interface.                  
                                                                                
  Use semantic HTML elements.                                                   
                                                                                
  Include the directory/master panel and the nested route content region.       
                                                                                
  Ensure child route content renders inside <Outlet />.                         
                                                                                
  Expected implementation pattern:                                              
                                                                                
  import {                                                                      
    NavLink,                                                                    
    Outlet                                                                      
  } from 'react-router';                                                        
                                                                                
  function PeopleLayout() {                                                     
    return (                                                                    
      <main className="people-layout">                                          
        <aside className="people-sidebar">                                      
          <nav aria-label="People directory">                                   
            {/* Existing directory navigation */}                               
          </nav>                                                                
        </aside>                                                                
                                                                                
        <section className="people-content">                                    
          <Outlet />                                                            
        </section>                                                              
      </main>                                                                   
    );                                                                          
  }                                                                             
                                                                                
  export default PeopleLayout;                                                  
  Adapt the master panel to the existing directory implementation rather than   
  creating duplicate data or navigation.                                        
                                                                                
  2.2 Nested Route Architecture                                                 
  Configure the parent route to render PeopleLayout.                            
                                                                                
  Expected structure:                                                           
                                                                                
  <Routes>                                                                      
    <Route                                                                      
      path="/people"                                                            
      element={<PeopleLayout />}                                                
    >                                                                           
      <Route                                                                    
        index                                                                   
        element={<PeopleDirectory />}                                           
      />                                                                        
                                                                                
      <Route                                                                    
        path=":id"                                                              
        element={<PersonDetail />}                                              
      />                                                                        
    </Route>                                                                    
  </Routes>                                                                     
  Requirements:                                                                 
                                                                                
  /people must render the directory's index view.                               
                                                                                
  /people/:id must render the selected person's detail view.                    
                                                                                
  Both child routes must share PeopleLayout.                                    
                                                                                
  Child content must render through <Outlet />.                                 
                                                                                
  Use the existing directory component if one already exists.                   
                                                                                
  2.3 Active Navigation Styling                                                 
  Use <NavLink> for navigation items.                                           
                                                                                
  Implement functional callback syntax for active styling:                      
                                                                                
  <NavLink                                                                      
    to="/people"                                                                
    className={({ isActive }) =>                                                
      isActive ? 'active' : ''                                                  
    }                                                                           
  >                                                                             
    People                                                                      
  </NavLink>                                                                    
  Requirements:                                                                 
                                                                                
  Active navigation must receive the active CSS class.                          
                                                                                
  Inactive navigation must not receive the active class.                        
                                                                                
  Avoid manually tracking the active route using separate React state.          
                                                                                
  2.4 Responsive CSS Grid                                                       
  Implement the required CSS Grid layout:                                       
                                                                                
  .people-layout {                                                              
    display: grid;                                                              
    grid-template-columns:                                                      
      minmax(280px, 1fr) 2fr;                                                   
  }                                                                             
  Requirements:                                                                 
                                                                                
  The master panel must have a minimum width of 280px.                          
                                                                                
  The detail panel must receive approximately twice the available width of the  
  master panel.                                                                 
                                                                                
  Avoid horizontal overflow on smaller screens.                                 
                                                                                
  Add an appropriate responsive breakpoint to stack the layout vertically when  
  necessary.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Example:                                                                      
                                                                                
  @media (max-width: 640px) {                                                   
    .people-layout {                                                            
      grid-template-columns: 1fr;                                               
    }                                                                           
  }                                                                             
  3. Acceptance Criteria                                                        
                                                                                
  PeopleLayout.jsx exists.                                                      
                                                                                
  Shared layout is implemented through nested routes.                           
                                                                                
  <Outlet /> renders child route content.                                       
                                                                                
  <NavLink> is used for navigation.                                             
                                                                                
  Functional className={({ isActive }) => ...} syntax works.                    
                                                                                
  CSS Grid uses minmax(280px, 1fr) 2fr.                                         
                                                                                
  Layout responds appropriately to mobile screen sizes.                         
                                                                                
  Existing directory navigation remains functional.                             
                                                                                
  No console warnings appear.                                                   
                                                                                
  4. Out of Scope                                                               
     Implementing the person lookup algorithm.                                  
                                                                                
  Implementing individual person data rendering.                                
                                                                                
  Implementing invalid-ID handling.

  production build succeeds.

  What changed:
  - Routes (src/App.jsx): /people uses PeopleLayout as the shared layout. Its
    index route shows PeopleDirectory and its :id route shows PersonDetail. I
    renamed the PRD 01 DirectoryIndex to PeopleDirectory to match the PRD.
  - Redirect (my addition): / now redirects to /people. Without it, opening the
    site root matches no route and React Router prints a warning, which the
    no-warnings rule forbids.
  - PeopleLayout.jsx: <main class="people-layout"> holds a header across the
    top, an <aside> with the "People directory" <nav>, and a <section> with
    <Outlet /> where the child routes appear. The nav has a "People" link plus
    one link per person, all built from the existing people data.
  - Active styling: each link uses className={({ isActive }) => (isActive ? 
    "active" : "")}. There's no useState tracking it. The "People" link has end,
    so it's only highlighted on /people itself and not while a person is open.
    Only one link is highlighted at a time.
  - Grid (PeopleLayout.css): grid-template-columns: minmax(280px, 1fr) 2fr,
    switching to one column at 640px and below.

  Tests:
  - Unit tests:
    - Both child routes render inside the same layout, and / redirects to
      /people.
    - The active class lands on the right link and moves when you click another
      one.
    - Any console warning or error fails the test.
    - Guard tests reject legacy router syntax and check the NavLink callback and
      the grid CSS.
  - Browser tests:
    - At 1280, 900 and 641px the two columns sit side by side, the list column
      is at least 280px, and the detail column is about twice as wide.
    - At 640, 375 and 320px the columns stack.
    - No width scrolls sideways, the browser back button updates the highlighted
      link, and the console stays clean.

  PersonDetail is still a placeholder: reading the id with useParams(), the
  people.find() lookup and the 404 page are out of scope here.

  Your move of Exercise 1.1 into EXERCISE/1.1 is still uncommitted; I left it
  out of this commit.

  Ready for the next PRD.
